Share How Iran messaging highlights cost of war ahead of US midterm elections on social media
Gas prices are seen at a gas station in Deerfield, Ill., Wednesday, Oct. 14, 2026. [Nam Huh/AP]
In messages that appear to be directed at the American public, Iran has been highlighting the cost of the war that the United States and Israel launched against the country ahead of the United States midterm elections.
Iranian officials have published articles in state-affiliated outlets stressing the disastrous impact of the war on oil prices and the US economy, while Iran’s chief negotiator and Parliament Speaker Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf has actively targeted US officials about rising costs and military spending.
list of 3 items list 1 of 3 Dozens of aircraft, hundreds of buildings: US loss to Iran attacks revealed
list 2 of 3 Iran war has cost the US $38bn: How will it impact US economy, politics?
list 3 of 3 What’s behind reports that the Israeli army is low on troops, equipment?
Sina Toossi, a senior non-resident fellow at the Center for International Policy, said Tehran is looking to increase the political cost of the war for Trump by trying to talk directly to the American public.
“It’s not necessarily that they’re trying to get Democrats in power or manipulate US politics in a certain direction, other than just overall increasing the costs on this issue of the Iran war, and just playing that game of information warfare and getting out their own narrative and propaganda as well,” Toossi told Al Jazeera.
Trump’s Republican Party is looking to retain control of the Senate and House of Representatives in next month’s midterm vote. But the Democratic Party, which largely opposes the war with Iran, is mounting a strong challenge across the congressional electoral map, including in areas that have been conservative strongholds for decades, such as Texas .
On Wednesday, Ghalibaf shared a news story on inflation rising to 3.7 percent last month along with a collage of negative headlines on the US economy.
The social media post also featured a comic strip from the Road Runner cartoon showing Wile E Coyote building a catapult that misfires and launches a boulder at him.
“Let’s ‘play the percentages’. Not in odds, but in the % increases,” Ghalibaf wrote in a message to US Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent, who has been leading the sanctions campaign against Iran.
Bessent said last month that he represents “the house”, likening the economy to a casino while addressing the national debt and bond market.
Iranian Parliament Speaker Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf arrives at Zurich Airport in Switzerland, June 20, 2026 [Handout/Iranian Parliament Speaker Office/West Asia News Agency via Reuters] Ghalibaf’s remark referred to the increase in inflation, underscoring that the cost of living keeps rising due to the war despite Bessent’s assertions.
Days earlier, he shared an image of a ghost shaped like a bell curve, with diesel prices rising and housing affordability and consumer confidence falling.
Since the start of the war on February 28, Iran has disrupted shipping in the Strait of Hormuz, sending energy prices soaring. In the US, the price of diesel is at an all-time record of $6.33 per gallon (3.8 litres), according to the American Automobile Association, and gas is $4.33, up from less than $3 before the war.
Iran has intensified its attacks on vessels around the strait in recent days after Trump and his aides repeatedly stressed that the waterway is under US control.
The uptick in energy prices has fuelled overall inflation in the economy, leading the US Federal Reserve to raise interest rates last month at a time when Trump has been calling for them to be cut to stimulate economic activity.
In a letter to the American public on September 29, Iran’s Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC) accused Trump of lying about the cost of the war and the state of the US economy, dismissing the president’s claims that oil prices would soon nosedive.
“Countries around the world have lost their faith in the US economy. Demand for American bonds has plummeted. In fact, countries are starting to offload their US bonds. Soon, the dam will break, and the economy will implode, causing, unfortunately, great pain for Americans for many many years,” the IRGC said.
“Americans need to wake up. They need to wrest control back from corrupt politicians. Now is the time, as the American political system is at its weakest.”
The US midterm elections are seen as a turning point in the conflict – not because of their potential outcome, but because of what Trump might do after the vote.
Trump has suggested that he would either strike a deal with Iran or return to a full-scale war after the November 3 legislative elections. He has also dismissed the idea that the vote will have any impact on his Iran policy.
“They’re waiting to see how I do in the midterm election. What they don’t realise is that I’m not running,” Trump said at the United Nations General Assembly last month.
“I gave absolutely no credence and will not give credence to the election. When it comes to Iran, it doesn’t even enter my mind. The only thing that does is that Iran will never have a nuclear weapon.”
If the Democrats gain control of Congress, they can push for measures to curb Trump’s ability to continue the war, including through withholding funding for the military campaign.
But Toossi said there is a debate within Iran on the potential impact of a Democratic victory in November.
While some point to foreign policy hawks among the Democrats to argue that little would change, others say a midterm defeat would make him politically vulnerable and likely constrain his war powers .
“I don’t think they’re hedging their bets either way, and I don’t think it necessarily affects their overall strategy of increasing the costs [of war] regardless of whatever happens in November,” Toossi told Al Jazeera.

### 52. ★★★☆☆ How the US-Iran war upended two critical global trade routes - CNN

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: GN Geopolitics
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-17T05:00:45+00:00
- **发生了什么**: The war between the United States and Iran has upended the global oil market by severely disrupting the flow of oil through two critical waterways: the Strait of Hormuz and, in recent weeks, the Bab al-Mandeb Strait.
Iran is now stepping up attacks on tankers transiting Hormuz and the Persian Gulf, curtailing the volume of oil exiting the region and keeping global crude prices north of $100 a barr

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

The war between the United States and Iran has upended the global oil market by severely disrupting the flow of oil through two critical waterways: the Strait of Hormuz and, in recent weeks, the Bab al-Mandeb Strait.
Iran is now stepping up attacks on tankers transiting Hormuz and the Persian Gulf, curtailing the volume of oil exiting the region and keeping global crude prices north of $100 a barrel.
There have been 12 reported attacks on vessels in or near Hormuz in the past week, according to the United Kingdom Maritime Trade Operations, which monitors maritime security in the region. And, earlier this week, two cargo ships sank after attacks by unknown projectiles in separate incidents.
Analysts say intensifying attacks by Iran are in response to the large quantities of oil that have managed to exit Hormuz in recent weeks. The US military has led an effective operation to ferry oil across the strait by assisting “dark” transits – tankers sailing with their transponders turned off and shuttling oil to ships waiting in the Gulf of Oman. Meanwhile, Iran continues to struggle to sail its own tankers through the waterway because of a US naval blockade.
The conflict, now in its eighth month, has also widened to include fighting between Saudi Arabia and Iran-backed Houthi militants in Yemen. The Houthis have targeted Saudi oil tankers transiting the Bab al-Mandeb Strait and, in recent days, launched deadly strikes on Riyadh’s main international airport.
Videos verified by CNN show Singapore-flagged container ship GFS Pride sinking after it was struck by “unknown projectiles” on October 13. Smoke is seen rising from the ship as it takes on water. Later videos and satellite images show cargo scattered in the water in the Strait of Hormuz.
Two cargo ships sunk in Strait of Hormuz as Iran ramps up attacks in run-up to midterms
The crisis will have long-term ramifications on the global oil market, say analysts.
Oil-importing countries increasingly understand they must “structurally reduce their oil consumption” to become less vulnerable to external shocks, Georg Zachmann, a senior fellow at think tank Bruegel, told CNN.
That, alongside other measures, could help “shield (them) from this increasingly uncertain world,” he said.
The Bab al-Mandeb Strait became more strategically important after the war disrupted the neighboring Strait of Hormuz.
At its peak, around 4.5 million barrels of crude per day were exported from the Saudi Red Sea port of Yanbu, with the bulk – about 3 million barrels per day – exiting via Bab al-Mandeb, according to Richard Bronze, co-founder of Energy Aspects. But those flows “collapsed” after the Houthi threat started escalating this summer, he said.
Johannes Rauball, senior crude analyst at Kpler, said that if the Houthis begin threatening ships from all countries, “This would really create another blow for crude markets. It would really push prices higher again, really tighten balances.”
But after an attack last month by drones from inside Iraq on Saudi Arabia’s East-West pipeline – which pumps crude toward the country’s Red Sea ports – Riyadh pivoted quickly, stepping up crude exports through the Strait of Hormuz via the US military-assisted shuttle operation.
US President Donald Trump said Iran was probably behind the September attack, with Iran-backed militia groups known to operate inside Iraq. Iran’s foreign ministry has “categorically denied” the accusation. The East-West pipeline is now back online, according to several reports .
Asian countries — the biggest buyers of Saudi oil — have borne the brunt of the crisis during the war, but they have adapted by cutting consumption . China has also greatly reduced its imports by tapping into its strategic reserves. The crisis has also pushed some Middle Eastern countries to explore new export routes through yet-to-be-built pipelines.
“The longer this conflict drags on, the less leverage Iran actually has, because other countries are able to create bypass routes,” Rauball, at Kpler, said.
CNN’s Tim Lister, Aida Karimi, Jessie Yeung, Isaac Lee, Teele Rebane and David Goldman contributed reporting.

China warns EU not to ‘fantasise’ on trade approach as Brussels confirms new ‘kill switch’
Latest flare-up suggests danger of trade war between two of the world’s three biggest economies has far from de-escalated
Published: 6:30am, 17 Oct 2026 Updated: 7:16am, 17 Oct 2026
Beijing warned Brussels not to “fantasise” that taking a hard line will force it into concessions, as the bloc confirmed plans for a gung-ho new kill switch that will allow Brussels to “immediately” kick China out of the single market.
Exactly a week after the two sides struck a surprise deal to restrict the volume of hybrid cars China ships to Europe , the flare-up suggests the threat of a trade war between two of the world’s three biggest economies has far from subsided.
“ The EU should not fantasise about gaining benefits through pressure, nor should it underestimate China’s determination and ability to safeguard its own interests,” read a Ministry of Commerce statement on Friday.
The statement was in response to reports from the South China Morning Post and other media that Brussels believed that tough talk from Europe had forced Beijing to make a deal.

### 54. ★★★☆☆ AI for award-winning microchip design security - University of Idaho

- **分类**: 🤖 AI/科技
- **来源**: GN AI

- **发生了什么**: Electrical engineering undergraduate Lloyd Alcorn takes first place at world’s largest chip design competition

The Universit

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Electrical engineering undergraduate Lloyd Alcorn takes first place at world’s largest chip design competition
Alcorn and Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering Assistant Professor Zain Ul Abideen took first place in a competition at the world’s largest conference focused on chip design and electronic design automation.
For nearly a month, Lloyd Alcorn did not touch grass.
The University of Idaho College of Engineering senior spent a significant portion of his summer inside, running computer operations to test, refine and compete in one of the world’s most competitive challenges combining AI and semiconductor design.
Alcorn and Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering Assistant Professor Zain Ul Abideen took first place in the GenAI-Guided Hardware IP Redaction and Red-Teaming Challenge, a security challenge at the 63rd Design Automation Conference (DAC), the world’s largest conference focused on chip design and electronic design automation.
Students entering the semiconductor field need to understand both how advanced chips are designed and how the intellectual property inside them can be protected. This competition gave Lloyd hands-on experience with generative AI, chip-design tools and adversarial security analysis on a real-world problem. That combination is rarely available at the undergraduate level. Zain Ul Abideen
Assistant professor, Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering
DAC brings together more than 5,000 researchers, engineers, industry professionals and students to explore the future of semiconductor technology, artificial intelligence and hardware design. As an undergraduate, Alcorn competed alongside teams from academia and industry.
“It was a chance to get my feet wet as an undergraduate student, and I had no idea experiences like this were even possible until I came to U of I,” Alcorn said.
The competition ran from June to July 2026. Students register to participate as red team members, acting as attackers and blue team members ready to defend their systems. Teams meet their objectives independently then submit final results for demonstration at the conference.
DAC brings together more than 5,000 researchers, engineers, industry professionals and students to explore the future of semiconductor technology, artificial intelligence and hardware design. Alcorn and Abideen created a hardware security system embedded within a microchip to obfuscate, or hide, critical information about the chip’s functionality and intellectual property. They also had to build the system without sacrificing the chip’s computing power or performance. The competition was held remotely, with awards presented during the conference.
Microchips made of semiconductor material pass through multiple manufacturing, packaging, testing and distribution facilities, creating opportunities for sensitive information about a chip’s design and capabilities to be exposed or stolen.
“Students entering the semiconductor field need to understand both how advanced chips are designed and how the intellectual property inside them can be protected,” Abideen said. “This competition gave Lloyd hands-on experience with generative AI, chip-design tools and adversarial security analysis on a real-world problem. That combination is rarely available at the undergraduate level.”
Over several summer weeks, Alcorn adapted and tested the protection workflow, verifying that the protected designs continued to operate correctly, and measuring the effects on chip power and timing. He also conducted an AI-assisted adversarial evaluation to determine what information an attacker might still be able to learn from the protected design.
The remote summer competition pitted both sides of hardware security against each other. Blue teams developed methods to conceal sensitive design information, while red teams used generative AI to test whether that information could be reconstructed.
“Research experience at the intersection of hardware design and security requires experienced faculty willing to be patient with someone still learning. That’s what I found at U of I,” Alcorn said.

</details>

Broadcom Falls 4% as CEO Hock Tan’s Capital Warning Hangs Over AI Chips; Marvell Drops 4%
Broadcom (AVGO) fell 3% and Marvell (MRVL) dropped 4% after Hock Tan warned capital shortages could become AI's next binding constraint.
NVIDIA's general-purpose accelerators serve far more customers than AVGO or MRVL, limiting its exposure if large cloud buyers cut AI spending.
Multi-year custom chip contracts support Broadcom's bull case, but a capital crunch at concentrated cloud customers could push expected revenue into future years.
Just released. Our analysts combed the entire stock market and named the ten best stocks to buy right now, and Broadcom didn't make the cut. Enter your email to see the names that beat AVGO. The report is free. Enter your email and see if any of your stocks made the cut.
A blunt warning about AI funding from one of the chip industry's most prominent executives is hitting custom AI chip stocks harder than the wider semiconductor group. Broadcom ( NASDAQ:AVGO ) stock has dropped 4%, recently trading at $332.10. Meanwhile, Marvell Technology ( NASDAQ:MRVL ) stock is also down 4% and trades at $231.12.
Justin Sullivan / Getty Images Shares of Broadcom and Marvell are trailing the funds that track their corners of the market. The iShares Semiconductor ETF ( NASDAQ:SOXX ) is down 2.7%, a softer decline than either Broadcom stock or Marvell stock. Large-cap tech is holding up better (though it's still selling off), with the Invesco QQQ Trust ( NASDAQ:QQQ ) down 2.3%.
MRVL Price Target — 24/7 Wall St. Hock Tan Flags Capital as AI's Next Bottleneck
Broadcom CEO Hock Tan spoke at an event in Singapore. Earlier this week, he stated that investors "set very extreme expectations only to find out that it's almost like they are in a casino." Tan also warned that a shortage of capital could overtake chips and memory as the AI industry's next constraint. That second remark carries the most weight for Broadcom stock and Marvell stock, because it points at the money behind AI data centers.
24/7 Wall St has helped investors make money for over two decades, and our top analysts just finished ranking the definitive Top 10 Stocks To Buy Now . Not the ten biggest companies. Not the ten everyone is arguing about. The ten best stocks to buy right now.
The report is free, and you can see why we think each stock is a top investment today.
Broadcom and Marvell design custom AI chips for a small number of large cloud customers under multi-year programs. That structure gives Broadcom and Marvell long visibility into demand, and it also ties each to a short list of buyers, so even one of those customers pulling back on funding could hurt Broadcom and Marvell more than it would a supplier with a wider customer base.
NVIDIA ( NASDAQ:NVDA ) makes general-purpose AI accelerators that run across a wide range of customers and workloads. Its approach contrasts with the custom programs at Broadcom and Marvell, where each chip is designed around one buyer's specific needs.
The Invesco QQQ Trust counts Broadcom, Marvell and NVIDIA among its holdings, alongside a much wider mix of large-cap names outside the chip industry. Such a spread makes the Invesco QQQ Trust a broader gauge of large-cap tech than the iShares Semiconductor ETF, and memory, equipment and software names in the fund respond to different drivers than custom chip designers like Broadcom and Marvell do.
The bullish argument for Broadcom depends on custom chip programs that customers sign years in advance. Those commitments give Broadcom a revenue path that one conference remark doesn't change. Custom designs also take years to develop, which raises the cost for any customer that wants to replace Broadcom with another supplier.
On the bear side, the risk for Broadcom is that the capital shortage Tan described reaches the customers paying for those chips. Large cloud buyers facing reduced funding could stretch out deployment schedules, and a concentrated customer list leaves Broadcom with fewer ways to offset that. Even a modest delay could push revenue Broadcom expected in one year into the next.
AVGO Analyst Ratings — 24/7 Wall St. Capital Takes Center Stage in the AI Chip Trade
Tan's warning moves the AI chip debate from hardware supply to customer funding. Broadcom and Marvell now face the question of whether their large cloud customers keep financing AI buildouts at the pace those multi-year programs assume. Any sign that cloud buyers are cutting spending plans could pressure Broadcom stock and Marvell stock again.
Both stocks are falling harder than both funds. That is a sign the market is treating the capital warning as a specific risk for custom chip designers. For investors who share the bull case, a moderate position size makes sense, given the uncertainty Tan raised.

Stock Market Today, Oct. 16: Tech Stocks Plunge as AI Software Valuations Reverse
The S&P 500 (SNPINDEX:^GSPC) fell 1.07% to 7,661 as AI-related software stocks faced a sharp reversal, while the Nasdaq Composite (NASDAQINDEX:^IXIC) plummeted 2.15% to 26,140 on broad technology selling, and the Dow Jones Industrial Average (DJINDICES:^DJI) slipped 0.34% to 52,002, outperforming its peers to record its second straight weekly gain.
Gold prices rose 0.43% to $4,187.90 as of U.S. market close, the 10-Year Treasury yield declined to 5.10%, and Energy led sector gainers while Communication Services paced the decliners, followed by significant weakness in Technology.
Alcoa (NYSE:AA) shares jumped 5.80% on a third-quarter earnings beat, while Fox Factory (NASDAQ:FOXF) plummeted after announcing an unplanned CEO departure and a lower financial outlook.
Palantir Technologies (NASDAQ:PLTR) entered correction territory as software valuations pulled back, and Ford (NYSE:F) gained 2% as investors rotated into defensive blue chip names.
Neocloud CoreWeave (NASDAQ:CRWV) ended the week down 25% after Goldman Sachs (NYSE:GS) released a note saying that hedge funds sold AI stocks at the second-fastest pace in over a year. The firm considered the move a "tactical rotation" away from smaller AI names (like CoreWeave) toward steadier AI names, such as the Magnificent Seven, or even non-AI stocks altogether.
Netflix (NASDAQ:NFLX) named former Disney (NYSE:DIS) chair Peter Rice its new chief content officer, but NFLX stock slid 3% anyway.
Lastly, Apple (NASDAQ:AAPL) announced that preorders for its foldable $1,999 iPhone Duo sold out within minutes today. While this looks like a promising development on the surface, some analysts and Apple experts think this may be more of a supply constraint than pure, unbridled demand for the next-gen iPhone.

### 57. ★★★☆☆ The Stock Market Is Doing Something for Only the 3rd Time in 156 Years, and History Is Clear a Crash May be Hiding in Plain Sight - The Motley Fool

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: GN Markets
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-16T13:30:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: The Stock Market Is Doing Something for Only the 3rd Time in 156 Years, and History Is Clear a Crash May be Hiding in Plain Sight&nbsp;&nbsp;The Motley Fool

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

The S&P 500 ( ^GSPC -1.09% ) has been on an impressive run since the bear market of 2022. Since hitting a bottom in October 2022, the index has more than doubled, gaining approximately 115%. This translates into an annualized return of roughly 21% over four years, more than twice its historical average of approximately 10% annually, including dividends.
Here&#x27;s the catch: Stock prices do not outperform their long-term averages indefinitely. Eventually, markets experience mean reversion, a process in which unusually strong returns are followed by periods of weaker performance. While this doesn&#x27;t guarantee a crash is imminent, one particular valuation indicator is flashing a warning investors should not ignore.
The S&P 500 CAPE ratio is entering dangerous territory
The cyclically adjusted price-to-earnings ratio (CAPE) measures the S&P 500&#x27;s price relative to its average inflation-adjusted earnings over the last 10 years. The point is to smooth out temporary earnings fluctuations, providing a more balanced perspective on whether stocks are expensive or fairly valued.
Historically, the CAPE ratio has averaged around 17.8. But as of October 2026, readings are hovering around 41. To appreciate how unusual this is, consider some history.
Before the 1929 stock market crash, the CAPE ratio approached 33. The Dow Jones Industrial Average ( ^DJI -0.34% ) subsequently lost 89% of its value between 1929 and 1932.
In more recent history, the CAPE ratio exceeded 40 in 1999 and peaked near 44 before the dot-com bubble collapse. From March 2000 through October 2002, the S&P 500 declined 49%, while the Nasdaq Composite ( ^IXIC -2.15% ) plunged 77%.
Today&#x27;s CAPE reading above 40 puts stock valuations in rare territory, suggesting the current market is historically expensive. Much like the internet revolution, generative AI promises to transform entire industries, leading investors to pay premiums for companies expected to dominate the future. But I see one important difference between today&#x27;s AI boom and the dot-com bubble.
During the late 1990s, investors poured unprecedented sums into speculative technology companies with little revenue, no profits, and untested business models. Today&#x27;s AI leaders are considerably different.
Nvidia ( NVDA -1.43% ) sells the graphics processing units (GPUs) that power much of the AI revolution. Despite its stock price appreciation over the last few years, Nvidia&#x27;s earnings have also expanded as demand for its chips continues to accelerate.
Meanwhile, Micron Technology ( MU -5.63% ) is benefiting from soaring demand for high bandwidth memory (HBM) and advanced DRAM solutions. Alphabet ( GOOGL -2.47% ) ( GOOG -2.32% ) has combined an established, highly profitable advertising business with an expanding cloud infrastructure and AI services ecosystem.
These businesses aren&#x27;t selling investors a vision of the future. They&#x27;re actually generating consistent AI-driven revenue and profits today. This is why their forward price-to-earnings (P/E) ratios look reasonable relative to what the broader market&#x27;s CAPE ratio might suggest.
The crack I see is that future growth in the AI landscape hinges on the overarching assumption that big tech will continue spending at historical levels on AI infrastructure. According to Morgan Stanley , capital expenditures (capex) among the five major hyperscalers -- Microsoft , Amazon , Alphabet, Meta Platforms , and Oracle -- are projected to approach $800 billion in 2026, climb to $1.2 trillion in 2027, and reach $1.4 trillion in 2028.
Those investments are an enormous opportunity for Nvidia, Micron, and other semiconductor suppliers. But they also create a financial opportunity cost. Every dollar AI hyperscalers spend building data centers is one that cannot also be used to fund share repurchases or dividend increases. If AI infrastructure investments fail to deliver attractive returns on aggressive timelines, Wall Street could eventually question whether these budgets were justified.
The combination of outsize gains over the last four years fueling historically elevated valuations suggests the market has less room for disappointment. This is why taking some profits right now could make sense. In particular, for investors whose portfolios have become heavily concentrated in AI-related technology stocks, trimming some of your biggest winners could prove wise.
Of course, selling everything would be a questionable decision. Instead, I&#x27;d consider maintaining exposure to AI through a more diversified cohort of businesses. Industrial companies stand to benefit from data center construction, electrical equipment, and automation, while energy companies can profit from rising demand for electricity and natural gas. Meanwhile, utilities should benefit from investments in power generation and transmission infrastructure.
The biggest mistake investors can make right now is assuming that historically expensive valuations mean a crash is right around the corner. The CAPE ratio is a useful indicator of long-term expected returns, but it does not have a track record of predicting precisely when corrections will occur.

### 59. ★★★☆☆ State regulators approve nearly $140M rate hike for Consumers Energy natural gas customers - ClickOnDetroit | WDIV Local 4

- **分类**: 🛢️ 能源
- **来源**: GN Energy
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-16T14:21:58+00:00
- **发生了什么**: State regulators approve nearly $140M rate hike for Consumers Energy natural gas customers&nbsp;&nbsp;<font color="#6

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

2 warnings and 9 advisories in effect for 11 regions in the area
State regulators approve nearly $140M rate hike for Consumers Energy natural gas customers
The Michigan Public Service Commission has approved nearly $800 million in rate hikes for Consumers Energy since 2020
State regulators approved a $139.2 million annual natural gas rate increase for Consumers Energy — down from the utility’s original request for $240 million
Starting Nov. 1, Consumers Energy customers will see a 4.3% rate increase on their natural gas bills, amounting to approximately $4 per month
Michigan Attorney General Dana Nessel intervened in the case, urging the Commission charged with approving the rate hike to “slash the request by more than half”
Consumers Energy customers in Michigan will be paying more on their utility bills starting next month after state regulators approved a 4.3% rate increase on Thursday.
Under the new rate — which begins on Nov. 1 — households will pay an average of $4 more for natural gas each month, according to the Michigan Public Service Commission (MPSC).
The latest rate hike will allow the utility to collect an additional $139.2 million per year from its 1.8 million natural gas customers across the state — a 42% reduction from the $240 million Consumers Energy initially requested. The MPSC also approved a 9.8% return on equity, the utility’s profit margin on capital investments.
The utility says the increase is necessary to support investments in the safety and reliability of its natural gas system, and to support projects to replace aging gas mains and service lines.
However, the Michigan Attorney General’s office says it found “several instances” within Consumers Energy’s rate hike application where the company “inflated capital spending projections and included for themselves unreasonable profit margins.”
Related->> Michigan lawmakers push to rein in energy rates, hold utility companies accountable
Michigan Attorney General Dana Nessel said in a news release on Thursday that she urged state regulators to slash Consumers’ original rate hike request by more than half, arguing that the company shouldn’t be permitted to request a rate hike above 3.5% due to the burden it places on families already facing rising energy costs.
“Utility customers across the State, whether they pay their bill to Consumers Energy or DTE, ask all the time when their energy bills will stop climbing, and it’s clear that the answer is never,” Nessel said. “For nearly eight years my office has worked doggedly to curb these utility rate hikes, and in turn the cases have only grown. Change must come to our current system of annual on-demand rate hikes and our regulatory structure, but the only changes we’ve seen are fatter rates and heavier bills.”
Nessel says recent rate hike requests from DTE and Consumers Energy have included “inappropriate” expenses such as “private jet travel for staff, millions in executive incentive compensation tied to maximizing shareholder returns, and other unsupported expenditures that could not be demonstrated to be reasonable or prudent.”
The MPSC says it did not approve costs that couldn’t be “sufficiently justified,” noting that it did not approve Consumers’ $7.9 million request for transmission pipeline integrity work, citing “a pattern of over-projecting of costs and under-spending on this program.”
State regulators have approved nearly $800 million in rate hikes for Consumers Energy since 2020, according to Nessel’s office. Other rate hike cases currently open before the MPSC include DTE’s electric rate hike request and Upper Peninsula Power Company’s electric rate hike request .
